One important thing I added is the finally requirement. That matters a lot. If Antigravity writes something like:

_botsPromise = aFetch(...);
return _botsPromise;

but never resets _botsPromise after an error, one failed request could leave the dashboard stuck reusing a rejected Promise.

So the safe pattern should effectively be:

if (_botsPromise) return _botsPromise;

_botsPromise = fetchSomething();

try {
  return await _botsPromise;
} finally {
  _botsPromise = null;
}

Send that prompt. After it finishes, don’t move to Priority 2 immediately. First, run the dashboard and check the Network tab together. We want to confirm those duplicate users, bots, and stats/v2 requests are actually gone before touching anything else.
</USER_REQUEST>

### Usage Example
```jsx

### Props
| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| color | string | "#5227FF" | Stroke/glow color. Any CSS color (hex, rgb, hsl). |
| speed | number | 1 | Animation speed multiplier (higher = faster). |
| chaos | number | 0.12 | Distortion intensity (0 = no distortion, higher = more chaotic). |
| borderRadius | number | 24 | Border radius in pixels for the electric border path. |
| className | string | — | Optional className applied to the root wrapper. |
| style | React.CSSProperties | — | Inline styles for the wrapper. |
| children | ReactNode | — | Content rendered inside the bordered container. |

<USER_REQUEST>
## Integrate the <Galaxy /> component from React Bits

You are helping integrate an open-source React component into an existing application.

### Component: Galaxy
### Variant: JavaScript + CSS
### Dependencies: ogl

---

### Usage Example
```jsx
import Galaxy from './Galaxy';

// Basic usage
<div style={{ width: '100%', height: '600px', position: 'relative' }}>
  <Galaxy />
</div>

// With custom prop values
<div style={{ width: '100%', height: '600px', position: 'relative' }}>
  <Galaxy 
    mouseRepulsion={true}
    mouseInteraction={true}
    density={1.5}
    glowIntensity={0.5}
    saturation={0.8}
    hueShift={240}
  />
</div>
```

### Props
| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| focal | [number, number] | [0.5, 0.5] | Sets the focal point of the galaxy effect as [x, y] coordinates from 0 to 1 |
| rotation | [number, number] | [1.0, 0.0] | Controls the rotation matrix of the galaxy as [x, y] rotation values |
| starSpeed | number | 0.5 | Controls the speed of star movement and animation |
| density | number | 1 | Controls the density of stars in the galaxy |
| hueShift | number | 140 | Shifts the hue of all stars by the specified degrees (0-360) |
| disableAnimation | boolean | false | When true, stops all time-based animations |
| speed | number | 1.0 | Global speed multiplier for all animations |
| mouseInteraction | boolean | true | Enables or disables mouse interaction with the galaxy |
| glowIntensity | number | 0.3 | Controls the intensity of the star glow effect |
| saturation | number | 0.0 | Controls color saturation of stars (0 = grayscale, 1 = full color) |
| mouseRepulsion | boolean | true | When true, stars are repelled by the mouse cursor |
| twinkleIntensity | number | 0.3 | Controls how much stars twinkle (0 = no twinkle, 1 = maximum twinkle) |
| rotationSpeed | number | 0.1 | Speed of automatic galaxy rotation |
| repulsionStrength | number | 2 | Strength of mouse repulsion effect when mouseRepulsion is enabled |
| autoCenterRepul
<truncated 8628 bytes>
seActiveFactor.value = smoothMouseActive.current;

      renderer.render({ scene: mesh });
    }
    animateId = requestAnimationFrame(update);
    ctn.appendChild(gl.canvas);

    function handleMouseMove(e) {
      const rect = ctn.getBoundingClientRect();
      const x = (e.clientX - rect.left) / rect.width;
      const y = 1.0 - (e.clientY - rect.top) / rect.height;
      targetMousePos.current = { x, y };
      targetMouseActive.current = 1.0;
    }

    function handleMouseLeave() {
      targetMouseActive.current = 0.0;
    }

    if (mouseInteraction) {
      ctn.addEventListener('mousemove', handleMouseMove);
      ctn.addEventListener('mouseleave', handleMouseLeave);
    }

    return () => {
      cancelAnimationFrame(animateId);
      window.removeEventListener('resize', resize);
      if (mouseInteraction) {
        ctn.removeEventListener('mousemove', handleMouseMove);
        ctn.removeEventListener('mouseleave', handleMouseLeave);
      }
      ctn.removeChild(gl.canvas);
      gl.getExtension('WEBGL_lose_context')?.loseContext();
    };
  }, [
    focal,
    rotation,
    starSpeed,
    density,
    hueShift,
    disableAnimation,
    speed,
    mouseInteraction,
    glowIntensity,
    saturation,
    mouseRepulsion,
    twinkleIntensity,
    rotationSpeed,
    repulsionStrength,
    autoCenterRepulsion,
    transparent
  ]);

  return <div ref={ctnDom} className="galaxy-container" {...rest} />;
}

```

### Component CSS
```css
.galaxy-container {
  width: 100%;
  height: 100%;
  position: relative;
}

```

### Integration Instructions
1. Install any listed dependencies.
2. Copy the component source into the appropriate directory in the project.
3. Import the CSS file alongside the component.
4. Import and render the component using the usage example above as a starting point.
5. Adjust props as needed for the specific use case — refer to the props table for all available options.

You are helping integrate an open-source React component into an existing application.

### Component: FallingText
### Variant: JavaScript + CSS
### Dependencies: matter-js

---

### Usage Example
```jsx
import FallingText from './FallingText';
  
<FallingText
  text={`React Bits is a library of animated and interactive React components designed to streamline UI development and simplify your workflow.`}
  highlightWords={["React", "Bits", "animated", "components", "simplify"]}
  highlightClass="highlighted"
  trigger="hover"
  backgroundColor="transparent"
  wireframes={false}
  gravity={0.56}
  fontSize="2rem"
  mouseConstraintStiffness={0.9}
/>
```

### Props
| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| text | string | — | The text content to display and eventually animate. |
| highlightWords | string[] | [] | List of words or substrings to apply a highlight style. |
| highlightClass | string | "highlighted" | CSS class name for highlighted words. |
| trigger | 'click' | 'hover' | 'auto' | 'scroll' | "click" | Defines how the falling effect is activated. |
| backgroundColor | string | "transparent" | Canvas background color for the physics world. |
| wireframes | boolean | false | Whether to render the physics bodies in wireframe mode. |
| gravity | number | 1 | Vertical gravity factor for the physics engine. |
| mouseConstraintStiffness | number | 0.2 | Stiffness for the mouse drag constraint. |
| fontSize | string | "1rem" | Font size applied to the text before it falls. |
| wordSpacing | string | "2px" | Horizontal spacing between each word. |

### Usage Example
```jsx
import FallingText from './FallingText';

  text={`React Bits is a library of animated and interactive React components designed to streamline UI development and simplify your workflow.`}

### Props
| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| text | string | — | The text content to display and eventually animate. |
| highlightWords | string[] | [] | List of words or substrings to apply a highlight style. |
| highlightClass | string | "highlighted" | CSS class name for highlighted words. |
| trigger | 'click' | 'hover' | 'auto' | 'scroll' | "click" | Defines how the falling effect is activated. |
| backgroundColor | string | "transparent" | Canvas background color for the physics world. |
| wireframes | boolean | false | Whether to render the physics bodies in wireframe mode. |
| gravity | number | 1 | Vertical gravity factor for the physics engine. |
| mouseConstraintStiffness | number | 0.2 | Stiffness for the mouse drag constraint. |
| fontSize | string | "1rem" | Font size applied to the text before it falls. |
| wordSpacing | string | "2px" | Horizontal spacing between each word. |

### Full Component Source
```jsx

import Matter from 'matter-js';

const FallingText = ({
  className = '',
  text = '',
  highlightWords = [],

  trigger = 'auto',

<truncated 4290 bytes>
f (render.canvas && canvasContainerRef.current) {
        // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
        canvasContainerRef.current.removeChild(render.canvas);
      }
      World.clear(engine.world);
      Engine.clear(engine);
    };
  }, [effectStarted, gravity, wireframes, backgroundColor, mouseConstraintStiffness]);

  const handleTrigger = () => {
    if (!effectStarted && (trigger === 'click' || trigger === 'hover')) {
      setEffectStarted(true);
    }
  };

  return (
    <div
      ref={containerRef}
      className={`falling-text-container ${className}`}
      onClick={trigger === 'click' ? handleTrigger : undefined}
      onMouseEnter={trigger === 'hover' ? handleTrigger : undefined}
      style={{
        position: 'relative',
        overflow: 'hidden'
      }}
    >
      <div
        ref={textRef}
        className="falling-text-target"
        style={{
          fontSize: fontSize,
          lineHeight: 1.4
        }}
      />
      <div ref={canvasContainerRef} className="falling-text-canvas" />
    </div>
  );
};

export default FallingText;

Sprint 2 has been approved.

Do NOT implement animation.

Do NOT implement the terminal UI.

Do NOT implement logo morphing.

Current objective:

Convert the segmented portrait into a monochrome SVG portrait using Floyd–Steinberg error diffusion.

Input

debug/06_subject_on_black.png

Pipeline

1. Resize to exactly 300×340.

2. Convert to grayscale.

3. Apply Floyd–Steinberg dithering.

4. Use serpentine scanning.

5. Produce a true 1-bit image.

6. Merge adjacent pixels into SVG path runs instead of creating one rectangle per pixel.

7. Use shape-rendering="crispEdges".

8. Produce a monochrome portrait.

Required outputs

debug/

07_grayscale.png

08_dither.png

09_svg_preview.png

output/

portrait.svg

Requirements

• No animation

• No terminal

• No logo morph

• No filters

• No gradients

• Clean OOP

• Configurable threshold

• Configurable inversion

• Configurable SVG scale

• Extensive logging

Success Criteria

The SVG portrait visually resembles the original photograph while remaining entirely vector based.

Implement only Sprint 3.

Sprint 4 – Dot Analysis Engine

Do NOT implement animation.

Do NOT generate the terminal UI.

Do NOT implement logo morphing.

Current objective:

Analyze every foreground dot produced by Sprint 3 and generate metadata that will later drive all animations.

Create:

src/
    dot_analysis.py

Requirements

1. Read the binary dither image (08_dither_adaptive.png).

2. Detect every foreground pixel.

3. Convert every foreground pixel into a Dot object.

The Dot object must contain:

class Dot:
    id: int
    x: int
    y: int
    brightness: int
    distance_from_center: float
    normalized_x: float
    normalized_y: float
    is_edge: bool
    neighbors: int
    cluster_id: int | None
    band_id: int | None
    animation_group: int | None

4. Compute:

- distance from portrait center
- normalized coordinates (0–1)
- number of neighboring dots
- whether the dot lies on an edge
- local density

5. Build connected clusters.

6. Assign every dot to a cluster.

7. Export all dots to:

output/dots.json

8. Produce debug visualizations:

debug/
10_clusters.png
11_density.png
12_edges.png

Architecture

- Use dataclasses.
- Use type hints.
- OOP only.
- Logging at every stage.
- No hardcoded paths.
- Configuration from config.py.

Do NOT animate anything.

Do NOT assign random groups yet.

Do NOT create SVG animations.

Only analyze the dots and build metadata.

Success Criteria

The entire portrait is represented as structured Dot objects that future animation modules can use without parsing the SVG.

An audit found that point IDs are local to each logo.

Example:

Logo1:
id = 1081

Logo2:
id = 1081

Logo3:
id = 1081

These IDs refer to different coordinates.

However, transition events only store:

{
  "from_point": ...,
  "target_point": ...
}

without specifying which logo the point belongs to.

This creates ambiguity when resolving target_point into coordinates.

↓

frame generation

↓

coordinate lookup

↓

renderer

    "target_point": target_id,
    "logo": logo_index
}

The renderer/frame generator must resolve coordinates using the explicit logo index instead of inference.

Your ONLY task is to reduce the SVG size while preserving identical visual output.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROBLEM
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

The current SVG is approximately 51 MB.

An audit identified the root cause:

SVGRenderer.generate() builds one global keyTimes array by sampling every event, then writes that same large keyTimes string into every animated dot's <animate> tag.

Each dot therefore serializes hundreds of timestamps even if it only changes a few times.

This duplication is responsible for the majority of the SVG size.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
TASK
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Modify src/renderer/svg_renderer.py only.

Generate keyTimes and values PER DOT instead of using one global timeline.

Each animated circle should serialize only the timestamps required for that dot's own events.

Do NOT redesign the renderer.

Do NOT change:
- animation timing
- event ordering
- compiler output
- traveller paths
- particle count
- easing
- SVG visual appearance

The output animation should look identical.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
VALIDATION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

2. Report:
   - Original SVG size
   - New SVG size

   Portrait → Dragon → NX → </> → Portrait

The Module 5 plan is approved in principle, but make the following mandatory architectural corrections before implementation.

1. APPROVED DEFAULTS
Use:
- Access token lifetime: 15 minutes
- Refresh token lifetime: 7 days
- Strict email verification for STUDENT and STAFF
- SUPER_ADMIN bootstrap remains auto-verified

2. V8 EMAIL VERIFICATION MIGRATION
V8 is approved.

Ensure email verification token consumption is atomic so the same token cannot be successfully consumed concurrently twice.

Store only a cryptographic hash of verification tokens.

Inspect whether we need an index supporting expiry cleanup.

3. REFRESH TOKEN ROTATION / FAMILY REVOCATION
Before implementation, inspect the ACTUAL existing refresh_tokens migration and entity.

The plan claims refresh-token family revocation, but do not claim this is possible unless the current schema actually supports identifying a token family or replacement chain.

If the current schema cannot securely support replay detection + family revocation, propose and apply the smallest necessary migration.

Do not silently downgrade the security model.

4. TOKEN GENERATION
Do not use UUID for refresh, email verification, or password-reset security tokens.

Use SecureRandom with at least 32 random bytes and a URL-safe encoding.

Store only token hashes in the database.

5. REFRESH COOKIE / CSRF
Do not describe a cookie-authenticated refresh endpoint as inherently immune to CSRF.

Implement appropriate protection using:
- HttpOnly
- Secure in production
- appropriate SameSite policy
- strict configured frontend origins/CORS
- Origin/Referer validation for cookie-authenticated state-changing auth operations where appropriate

Cookie configuration must be environment-aware because local React development and production deployment may differ.

Never use wildcard CORS origins with credentials enabled.

6. JWT SECURITY
Validate JWT_SECRET strength at startup.

Use an adequately strong secret for the selected HMAC algorithm.

JWT claims should contain only the minimu
<truncated 940 bytes>
ership/department checks rather than one overly permissive generic method.

Repository/service queries should prefer tenant-scoped lookups where possible instead of fetching globally and checking afterward.

9. EMAIL VERIFICATION API
Add the missing endpoint:

POST /api/auth/resend-verification

It must:
- return a generic response
- avoid account enumeration
- invalidate or supersede old active verification tokens appropriately
- be designed for future rate limiting

10. PASSWORD RESET
Forgot-password must always return a generic response regardless of whether the account exists.

Successful password reset must revoke all existing refresh tokens for that user.

Token consumption must be one-time and safe against concurrent reuse.

11. LOGOUT

An absent, expired, invalid, or already-revoked refresh cookie should still safely clear the cookie without leaking token/account state.

- duplicate/concurrent email verification token consumption

A student's current semester represents their present academic state.

It must NOT determine the semester of an uploaded historical marksheet.

Example:

A student currently in Semester 6 may upload a Semester 1 marksheet.

The future marksheet pipeline must associate each marksheet independently with its own semester.

Inspect the existing marksheets table now and confirm exactly how marksheet -> semester is represented.

If marksheets already contain semester_id or equivalent, explicitly document that relationship.

If they do not, flag this as a genuine schema gap now rather than claiming the schema is fully sufficient.

Do not create a migration yet unless it is necessary for Module 8, but document the exact requirement for the marksheet module.

subjects

verifications

model if the current academic schema supports different fields.

For example, distinguish:

- value absent from document
- value unreadable
- provider returned null
- provider returned an invalid value

Do not invent academic values.

3. NEVER CAP INVALID MARKS

"Mark exceeds maxMarks (marks will be capped/rejected)"

CAPPING IS FORBIDDEN.

If OCR extracts 180 for a subject whose maximum is 100:

AI/OCR must never silently alter academic marks to make them valid.

1. Exact normalized subject-code match
2. Exact normalized subject-name match

- missing expected subject
- unknown extra subject

- null/unreadable marks

- provider rate limit

- failed reprocessing does not leave partial subject rows
